What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… mostly work stuff. I keep leaving my station when I’m supposed to stay there, or I pop up in meetings without really meaning to. My supervisor said it’s starting to be a safety issue because I just kind of take off before I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. Like at work, I’ll just leave my station or stand up in meetings when I’m supposed to stay put. I don’t really think it through first, I just kind of go, and my supervisor says it’s becoming a safety problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to details or following through on instructions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Like with inventory logs, or those CNC training pages, I’ll start off trying to pay attention, but after a few minutes the numbers or words just kind of run together. Then I realize I didn’t really take it in and have to go back over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah. When I’m supposed to just stand by the machine, or sit through training, I get this… like pressure in my body. I feel like I have to move or stretch or pace, just do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble sitting still and paying attention, or is this something that started more recently?\n\nPatient: No, it’s been like that a long time. Even in elementary school, I’d get in trouble for not staying in my seat… talking over the teacher, blurting stuff out. Sometimes I’d just be wandering around the room, and they’d send notes home.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your impulsive behaviors or trouble paying attention are causing problems for you in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it is. Mostly at work. I’ve gotten written up for safety stuff and production mistakes, and it’s starting to mess with my certification plans. Some coworkers don’t really want to partner with me on handoffs anymore, which… yeah, that’s been hard.\n\nDoctor: Did you have a lot of energy or feel restless when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. In elementary school I was always getting told to sit down. I’d talk over the teacher or blurt stuff out, sometimes wander around, and notes would get sent home.\n\nDoctor: Do you also feel restless or have trouble sitting still when you're not at work or in a classroom?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s not just work or school stuff. If I’m trying to stand still somewhere, or sit through training, it’s like this pressure builds up in my body. I feel like I have to move or pace or stretch… do something right then.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sitting still or feel restless when you're at home or doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I notice it more with stuff like standing by the machine or sitting through training. It’s like this pressure in my body, like I need to move or pace or stretch right then. At home it can be kind of like that too, just… hard to stay still.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these impulsivity and restlessness symptoms consistently since childhood?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much. Since I was little, like elementary school, I was always the kid who couldn’t stay in my seat. I’d talk over the teacher or blurt stuff out, and I got notes sent home for wandering around the room and stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're relaxing or doing leisure activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it happens. Even if I’m supposed to be just relaxing, I get that feeling like pressure building up and I need to move around or stretch or do something. It’s hard to just stay still.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or have trouble staying still when you're doing things you enjoy, like hobbies?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even with hobbies, I can’t really stay still. It’s that same feeling, like pressure building up in my body, and I have to get up, move around, stretch, or go do something right then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often make careless mistakes or overlook details in your work or daily activities?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know… I mean, I haven’t paid that much attention to that. Maybe sometimes, but I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these symptoms are causing you a lot of stress or distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does. Work’s been the main thing. I’ve gotten written up for safety and production stuff, and now some people don’t really want to partner with me on handoffs. It’s stressful because it could mess up my job… and the certification I’m trying to get.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble staying still or feel restless in quiet activities like reading or watching a movie?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Quiet stuff kind of makes me feel… stuck, I guess. Like if I try to sit in the break room and read the manual, I’ll start tapping or humming, or I end up talking to somebody or just getting up. I don’t really just sit there quiet.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
